[that children eat their carrots before they get]{.s-t1} 

[dessert. And the business version requires that]{.s-t1} 

[executives force themselves daily to first do their]{.s-t1} 

[unpleasant and necessary tasks before rewarding themselves by proceeding to their pleasant tasks.]{.s-t1} 

[Given reward superpower, this practice is wise and sound. Moreover. the rule can also be used in the nonbusiness part of life. The emphasis on daily use of this practice is not accidental. The consultants well know after the teaching of Skinner, that]{.s-t1} 

[prompt rewards work best.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Punishments, of course, also strongly influence behavior and cognition, although not so flexibly and wonderfully as rewards. For instance, illegal price fixing was fairly common in America when it was customarily punished by modest fines. Then, after]{.s-t1} 

[a few prominent business executives were removed]{.s-t1} 

[from their eminent positions and sent to federal]{.s-t1} 

[prisons, price-fixing behavior was greatly reduced.]{.s-t1}

[extreme behavior. Around the time of Caesar, there]{.s-t1} 

[was a European tribe that, when the assembly horn]{.s-t1}

[who might contemplate desertion.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Two: Liking/Loving Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[A newly hatched baby goose is programmed,]{.s-t1} 

[through the economy of its genetic program, to]{.s-t1} 

[\"love\" and follow the first creature that is nice to it, which is almost always its mother. But, if the mother goose is not present right after the hatching, and a man is there instead, the gosling will \"love\"]{.s-t1} 

[and follow the man. who becomes a sort of substitute mother.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Somewhat similarly, a newly arrived human]{.s-t1} 

[is \"born to like and love\" under the normal and abnormal triggering outcomes for its kind. Perhaps the strongest inborn tendency is love ready to be triggered-is that of the human mother for its child. On the other hand, the similar \"child-loving\"]{.s-t1} 

[behavior of a mouse can be eliminated by the deletion of a single gene, which suggests there is some sort of triggering gene in a mother mouse as]{.s-t1} 

[well as in a gosling.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Each child, like a gosling, will almost surely]{.s-t1} 

[come to like and love, not only as driven by its sexual nature, but also in social groups not limited to its genetic or adoptive \"family.\" Current extremes of romantic love almost surely did not]{.s-t1} 

[occur in man\'s remote past. Our early human ancestors were surely more like apes triggered into]{.s-t1} 

[mating in a pretty mundane fashion.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And what will a man naturally come to like and love, apart from his parent, spouse and child? Well, he will like and love being liked and loved. And so many a courtship competition will be won by a]{.s-t1} 

[person displaying exceptional devotion, and man will generally strive, lifelong, for the affection and approval of many people not related to him.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One very practical consequence of Liking/ Loving Tendency is that it acts as a conditioning device that makes the liker or lover tend (l) to ignore faults oi and comply with wishes of, the object of his affection, (2) to favor people, products, and actions merely associated with the object]{.s-t1} 

[of his affection (as we shall see when we get to \"]{.s-t1}[Influence]{.s-t7}[-from-Mere-Association TendencY,\" and (3) to distort other facts to facilitate love.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The phenomenon of liking and loving causing]{.s-t1} 

[admiration also works in reverse. Admiration]{.s-t1} 

[also causes or intensifies liking or love. With this \"feedback mode\" in place, the consequences are]{.s-t1} 

[often extreme, sometimes even causing deliberate]{.s-t1} 

[self-destruction to help what is loved.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Liking or loving, intertwined with admiration in a feedback mode, often has vast practical consequences in areas far removed from sexual attachments. For instance, a man who is so constructed]{.s-t1} 

[that he loves admirable persons and ideas with a special intensity has a huge advantage in life. This blessing came to both Buffett and myself in large]{.s-t1} 

[measure, sometimes from the same persons and]{.s-t1} 

[ideas. One common, beneficial example for us both]{.s-t1} 

[was Warren\'s uncle, Fred Buffett, who cheerfully did the endless grocery-store work that Warren and I ended up admiring from a safe distance. Even now, after I have known so many other people, I doubt if it is possible to be a nicer man than Fred Buffett was, and he changed me for the better.]{.s-t1} 

 

[There are large social policy implications in the]{.s-t1} 

[amazingly good consequences that ordinarily come from people likely to trigger extremes of love and]{.s-t1} 

[admiration boosting each other in a feedback mode. For instance, it is obviously desirable to attract a lot of lovable, admirable people into the teaching profession.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Three: Disliking/Hating Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[In a pattern obverse to Liking/Loving]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency, the newly arrived human is also \"born]{.s-t1} 

[to dislike and hate\" as triggered by normal and abnormal triggering forces in its life. It is the same with most apes and monkeys.]{.s-t1} 

 

[As a result, the long history of man contains]{.s-t1} 

[almost continuous war. For instance, most]{.s-t1} 

[American Indian tribes warred incessantly, and]{.s-t1} 

[some tribes would occasionally bring captives home to women so that all could join in the fun of torturing captives to death. Even with the spread]{.s-t1} 

[of religion, and the advent of advanced civilization,]{.s-t1} 

[much modern war remains pretty savage. But we]{.s-t1} 

[also get what we observe in present-day Switzerland]{.s-t1} 

[and the United States, wherein the clever political]{.s-t1} 

[arrangements of man \"channel\" the hatreds and]{.s-t1} 

[dislikings of individuals and groups into nonlethal patterns including elections.]{.s-t1} 

 

[But the dislikings and harreds never go away]{.s-t1} 

[completely. Born into man, these driving tendencies remain strong. Thus, we get maxims like the]{.s-t1} 

[one from England: \"Politics is the art of marshalling]{.s-t1} 

[hatreds.\" And we also get the extreme popularity of very negative political advertising in the United]{.s-t1} 

[States.]{.s-t1} 

 

[At the family level, we often see one sibling]{.s-t1} 

[hate his other siblings and litigate with them endlessly if he can afford it. Indeed, a wag named Buffett has repeatedly explained to me that \"a major difference between rich and poor people is]{.s-t1} 

[that the rich people can spend their lives suing their]{.s-t1} 

[relatives.\" My father\'s law practice in Omaha was full of such intrafamily hatreds. And when I got to]{.s-t1} 

[the Harvard Law School and its professors taught]{.s-t1} 

[me \"property law\" with no mention of sibling rivalry in the family business, I appraised the School as a pretty unrealistic place that wore \"blinders\" like the milk-wagon horses of yore. My current guess is that sibling rivalry has not yet made it into]{.s-t1} 

[property law as taught at Harvard.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Disliking/Hating Tendency also acts as a conditioning device that makes the disliker hater tend to (1) ignore virtues in the object of dislike, (2) dislike]{.s-t1} 

[people, products, and actions merely associated]{.s-t1} 

[with the object of his dislike, and (3) distort other]{.s-t1} 

[facts to facilitate hatred.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Distortion of that kind is often so extreme that]{.s-t1} 

[miscognition is shockingly large. When the World]{.s-t1} 

[Trade Center was destroyed, many Pakistanis]{.s-t1} 

[immediately concluded that the Hindus did it, while many Muslims concluded that the Jews did it. Such factual distortions often make mediation between opponents locked in hatred either difficult or impossible. Mediations between Israelis and Palestinians are difficult because facts in one side\'s history overlap very little with facts from the other side\'s]{.s-t1} 

[]{#index_split_000.xhtml_anchor28 .calibre2} 

[Four: Doubt-Avoidance Tendency.]{.s-t14} 

 

[The brain of man is programmed with a tendency to quickly remove doubt by reaching]{.s-t1} 

[some decision.]{.s-t1} 

 

[It is easy to see how evolution would make animals, over the eons, drift toward such quick]{.s-t1} 

[elimination of doubt. After all, the one thing that]{.s-t1} 

[is surely counterproductive for a prey animal that]{.s-t1} 

[is threatened by a predator is to take a long time]{.s-t1} 

[in deciding what to do. And so man\'s Doubt]{.s-t1} 

[Avoidance Tendency is quite consistent with the]{.s-t1} 

[history of his ancient, non human ancestors.]{.s-t1} 

 

[So pronounced is the tendency in man to]{.s-t1} 

[quickly remove doubt by reaching some decision]{.s-t1} 

[that behavior to counter the tendency is required]{.s-t1} 

[from judges and jurors. Here, delay before decision]{.s-t1} 

[making is forced. And one is required to so comport]{.s-t1} 

[himself, prior to conclusion time, so that he is]{.s-t1} 

[wearing a \"mask\" of objectivity. And the \"mask\"]{.s-t1} 

[works to help real objectivity along, as we shall see when we next consider man\'s Inconsistency Avoidance \'tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Of course, once one has recognized that man has a strong Doubt-Avoidance Tendency, it is logical]{.s-t1} 

[to believe that at least some leaps of religious faith]{.s-t1} 

[are greatly boosted by this tendency. Even if one is]{.s-t1} 

[satisfied that his own faith comes from revelation,]{.s-t1} 

[one still must account for the inconsistent faiths of]{.s-t1} 

[others. And man\'s Doubt-Avoidance Tendency is]{.s-t1} 

[almost surely a big part of the answer.]{.s-t1} 

 

[What triggers Doubt-Avoidance Tendency?]{.s-t1} 

[Well, an unthreatened man, thinking of nothing in]{.s-t1} 

[particular, is not being prompted to remove doubt]{.s-t1} 

[through rushing to some decision. As we shall see]{.s-t1} 

[later when we get to Social-Proof Tendency and]{.s-t1} 

[Stress-Influence Tendency, what usually triggers]{.s-t1} 

[Doubt-Avoidance Tendency is some combination of (l) puzzlement and (Z) stress. And both of these]{.s-t1} 

[factors naturally occur in facing religious issues.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Thus, the natural state of most men is in some]{.s-t1} 

[form of religion. And this is what we observe.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Five: Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[The brain of man conserves programming space]{.s-t1} 

[by being reluctant to change, which is a form of]{.s-t1} 

[inconsistency avoidance. We see this in all human]{.s-t1} 

[habits, constructive and destructive. Few people]{.s-t1} 

[can list a lot of bad habits that they have eliminated,]{.s-t1} 

[and some people cannot identify even one of these. Instead, practically everyone has a great many bad]{.s-t1} 

[habits he has long maintained despite their being known as bad. Given this situation, it is not too]{.s-t1} 

[much in many cases to appraise early-formed habits as destiny. When Marley\'s miserable ghost says, \"I wear the chains I forged in life,\" he is talking about]{.s-t1} 

[chains of habit that were too light to be felt before]{.s-t1} 

[they became too strong to be broken.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The rare life that is wisely lived has in it many]{.s-t1} 

[good habits maintained and many bad habits]{.s-t1} 

[avoided or cured. And the great rule that helps here]{.s-t1} 

[is again from Franklin\'s Poor Richard\'s Almanac: \"An ounce of prevention is worth a pound of cure.\" What Franklin is here indicating, in part, is that Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency makes it much easier to prevent a habit than to change it.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Also tending to be maintained in place by the]{.s-t1} 

[anti-change tendency of the brain are one\'s previous]{.s-t1} 

[conclusions, human loyalties, reputational identity, commitments, accepted role in a civilization, etc. It is not entirely clear why evolution would program into man\'s brain an anti-change mode alongside his tendency to quickly remove doubt. My guess is]{.s-t1} 

[the anti-change mode was significantly caused by a]{.s-t1} 

[combination of the following factors:]{.s-t1} 

 

[(1) It facilitated faster decisions when speed]{.s-t1} 

[of decision was an important contribution to the]{.s-t1} 

[survival of nonhuman ancestors that were prey.]{.s-t1} 

 

[(2) It facilitated the survival advantage that our ancestors gained by cooperating in groups, which]{.s-t1} 

[would have been more difficult to do if everyone]{.s-t1} 

[was always changing responses.]{.s-t1} 

 

[(3) It was the best form of solution that evolution could get to in the limited number of]{.s-t1} 

[generations between the start of literacy and today\'s]{.s-t1} 

[complex modern life.]{.s-t1} 

 

[It is easy to see that a quickly reached conclusion, triggered by Doubt-Avoidance Tendency,]{.s-t1} 

[when combined with a tendency to resist any change in that conclusion, will natUrally cause a lot of errors in cognition for modern man. And so it observably works out. We all deal much with others whom we correctly diagnose as imprisoned in poor]{.s-t1} 

[conclusions that are maintained by mental habits they formed early and will carry to their graves.]{.s-t1} 

 

[So great is the bad-decision problem caused by]{.s-t1} 

[Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency that our courts have adopted important strategics against it. For instance, before making decisions, judges and juries are required to hear long and skillful presentations of evidence and argument from the side they will not naturally favor, given their ideas in place. And]{.s-t1} 

[this helps prevent considerable bad thinking from]{.s-t1} 

[\"first conclusion bias.\" Similarly, other modern]{.s-t1} 

[decision makers will often force groups to consider]{.s-t1} 

[skillful counter arguments before making decisions.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And proper education is one long exercise in]{.s-t1} 

[augmentation of high cognition so that our wisdom becomes strong enough to destroy wrong thinking maintained by resistance to change. As Lord]{.s-t1} 

[Keynes pointed out about his exalted intellectual]{.s-t1} 

[group at one of the greatest universities in the world, it was not the intrinsic difficulty of new ideas]{.s-t1} 

[that prevented their acceptance. Instead, the new]{.s-t1} 

[ideas were not accepted because they were inconsistent with old ideas in place. What Keynes was reporting is that the human mind works a lot like the human egg. When one sperm gets into a human]{.s-t1} 

[egg, there\'s an automatic shut-off device that bars any other sperm from getting in. The human mind]{.s-t1} 

[tends strongly toward the same sort of result.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And so, people tend to accumulate large mental]{.s-t1} 

[holdings of fixed conclusions and attitudes that]{.s-t1} 

[are not often reexamined or changed, even though]{.s-t1} 

[there is plenty of good evidence that they are]{.s-t1} 

[wrong.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Moreover, this doesn\'t just happen in social]{.s-t1} 

[science departments, like the one that once]{.s-t1} 

[thought Freud should serve as the only choice as]{.s-t1} 

[a psychology teacher for Caltech. Holding to old errors even happens, although with less frequency]{.s-t1} 

[and severity, in hard science departments. We have no less an authority for this than Max Planck, Nobel]{.s-t1} 

[laureate, finder of \"Planck\'s constant.\" Planck is]{.s-t1} 

[famous not only for his science but also for saying]{.s-t1} 

[that even in physics the radically new ideas are seldom really accepted by the old guard. Instead,]{.s-t1} 

[said Planck, the progress is made by a new generation that comes along, less brain-blocked by its previous conclusions. Indeed, precisely this sort of brain-blocking happened to a degree in Einstein. At his peak, Einstein was a great destroyer of his]{.s-t1} 

[own ideas, but an older Einstein never accepted the full implications of quantum mechanics.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One of the most successful users of an antidote]{.s-t1} 

[to first conclusion bias was Charles Darwin. He]{.s-t1} 

[trained himself, early, t\<t intensively consider any]{.s-t1} 

[evidence tending to disconfirm any hypothesis of his, more so if he thought his hypothesis was a particularly good one. The opposite of what]{.s-t1} 

[Darwin did is now called confirmation bias, a term of opprobrium. Darwin\'s practice came from his]{.s-t1} 

[acute recognition of man\'s natural cognitive faults]{.s-t1} 

[arising from Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

[He provides a great example of psychological]{.s-t1} 

[insight correctly used to advance some of the finest]{.s-t1} 

[mental work ever done.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency has many good effects in civilization. For instance, rather]{.s-t1} 

[than act inconsistently with public commitments, new or old public identities, etc., most people are]{.s-t1} 

[more loyal in their roles in life as priests, physicians,]{.s-t1} 

[citizens, soldiers, spouses, teachers, employees, etc.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One corollary of Inconsistency-Avoidance]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency is that a person making big sacrifices in the course of assuming a new identity will intensify his devotion to the new identity. After all, it would be quite inconsistent behavior to make a large]{.s-t1} 

[sacrifice for something that was no good. And thus civilization has invented many tough and solemn]{.s-t1} 

[initiation ceremonies, often public in nature, that intensify new commitments made.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Tough initiation ceremonies can intensify bad contact as well as good. The loyalty of the new, \"made-man\" mafia member of the military]{.s-t1} 

[officer making the required \"blood oath\" of loyalty to Hitler, was boosted through the triggering of]{.s-t1} 

[Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Moreover, the tendency will often make man a \"patsy\" of manipulative \"compliance-practitioners,\" who gain advantage from triggering his]{.s-t1} 

[subconscious Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

[Few people demonstrated this process better than Ben Franklin. As he was rising from obscurity in Philadelphia and wanted the approval of some]{.s-t1} 

[important man, Franklin would often maneuver]{.s-t1} 

[that man into doing Franklin some unimportant favor like lending Franklin a book. Thereafter the]{.s-t1} 

[man would admire and trust Franklin more because]{.s-t1} 

[a non admitted and non trusted Franklin would be inconsistent with the appraisal implicit in lending]{.s-t1} 

[Franklin the book.]{.s-t1} 

 

[During the Korean War, this technique of Franklin\'s was the most important feature of the]{.s-t1} 

[Chinese brainwashing system that was used on]{.s-t1} 

[enemy prisoners. Small step by small step, the]{.s-t1} 

[technique often worked better than torture in]{.s-t1} 

[altering prisoner cognition in favor of Chinese]{.s-t1} 

[captors.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The practice of Franklin, whereunder he got]{.s-t1} 

[approval from someone by maneuvering him into treating Franklin favorably, works viciously well in]{.s-t1} 

[reverse. When one is maneuvered into deliberately]{.s-t1} 

[hurting some other person, one will tend to disapprove or even hate that person. This effect, from]{.s-t1} 

[Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency, accounts for]{.s-t1} 

[the insight implicit in the saying: \"A man never]{.s-t1} 

[forgets where he has buried the hatchet.\" The]{.s-t1} 

[effect accounts for much prisoner abuse by gerards,]{.s-t1} 

[increasing their dislike and hatred for prisoners]{.s-t1} 

[that exists as a consequence of the guards\' reciprocation of hostility from prisoners who are treated]{.s-t1} 

[like animals. Given the psychology-based hostility]{.s-t1} 

[natural in prisons between guards and prisoners, an intense, continuous effort should be made (1) to]{.s-t1} 

[prevent prisoner abuse from starting and (2) to stop]{.s-t1} 

[it instantly when it starts because it will grow by]{.s-t1} 

[feeding on itself, like a cluster of infectious disease.]{.s-t1} 

[More psychological acuity on this subject, aided by]{.s-t1} 

[more insightful teaching, would probably improve]{.s-t1} 

[the overall effectiveness of the U.S. Army.]{.s-t1} 

 

[So strong is Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency]{.s-t1} 

[that it will often prevail after one has merely]{.s-t1} 

[pretended to have some identity habit, or conclusion. Thus, for a while, many an actor sort of]{.s-t1} 

[believes he is Hamlet, Prince of Denmark. And]{.s-t1} 

[many a hypocrite is improved by his pretensions of virtue. And many a judge and juror, while]{.s-t1} 

[pretending objectivity, is gaining objectivity. And]{.s-t1} 

[many a trial lawyer or other advocate comes to]{.s-t1} 

[believe what he formerly only pretended to believe.]{.s-t1} 

 

[While Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency,]{.s-t1} 

[with its \"status quo bias,\" immensely harms sound education. it also causes much benefit. For]{.s-t1} 

[instance, a near-ultimate inconsistency would be to]{.s-t1} 

[teach something to others that one did not believe]{.s-t1} 

[true. And so, in clinical medical education, the]{.s-t1} 

[learner is forced to \"see one, do one, and then teach]{.s-t1} 

[one,\" with the teaching pounding the learning into]{.s-t1} 

[the teacher. Of course, the power of teaching to]{.s-t1} 

[influence the cognition of the teacher is not always]{.s-t1} 

[a benefit to society. When such power flows into]{.s-t1} 

[political and cult evangelism, there are often bad]{.s-t1} 

[consequences.]{.s-t1} 

 

[For instance, modern education often does]{.s-t1} 

[much damage when young students are taught]{.s-t1} 

[dubious political notions and then enthusiastically push these notions on the rest of us. The pushing]{.s-t1} 

[seldom convinces others. But as students pound]{.s-t1} 

[into their mental habits what they are pushing]{.s-t1} 

[out, the students are often permanently damaged.]{.s-t1} 

[Educational institutions that create a climate where much of this goes on are, I think, irresponsible. It]{.s-t1} 

[is important not to thus put one\'s brain in chains before one has come anywhere near his full potentiality as a rational person.]{.s-t1} 

 

[S]{.s-t4}[i]{.s-t11}[x: Curiosity]{.s-t4}[ Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[There is a lot of innate curiosity in mammals,]{.s-t1} 

[but it\'s nonhuman version is highest among apes and]{.s-t1} 

[monkeys. Man\'s curiosity, in turn, is much stronger]{.s-t1} 

[than that of his simian relatives. In advanced]{.s-t1} 

[human civilization, culture greatly increases the]{.s-t1} 

[effectiveness of curiosity in advancing knowledge. For instance, Athens (including its colony, Alexandria) developed much math and science our of pure curiosity while the Romans made almost no]{.s-t1} 

[contribution to either math or science. They instead]{.s-t1} 

[concentrated their attention on the \"practical\" engineering of mines, roads, aqueducts, etc. Curiosity, enhanced by the best of modern education (which]{.s-t1} 

[is by definition a minority part in many places;,]{.s-t1} 

[much helps man to prevent or reduce bad consequences arising from other psychological tendencies. The curious are also provided with much fun]{.s-t1} 

[and wisdom long after formal education has ended.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Seven: Kantian Fairness Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[Kant was famous for his \"categorical imperative,\" a sort of a \"golden rule\" that required humans to follow those behavior patterns that, if followed]{.s-t1} 

[by all others, would make the surrounding human]{.s-t1} 

[system work best for everybody. And it is not too]{.s-t1} 

[much to say that modern acculturated man displays,]{.s-t1} 

[and expects from others, a lot of fairness as thus]{.s-t1} 

[defined by Kant.]{.s-t1} 

 

[In a small community having a one-way bridge]{.s-t1} 

[or tunnel for autos, it is the norm in the United]{.s-t1} 

[States to see a lot of reciprocal courtesy, despite]{.s-t1} 

[the absence of signs or signals. And many freeway]{.s-t1} 

[drivers, including myself, will often let other drivers]{.s-t1} 

[come in front of them, in lane changes or the like,]{.s-t1} 

[because that is the courtesy they desire when roles]{.s-t1} 

[are reversed. Moreover, there is, in modern human]{.s-t1} 

[culture, a lot of courteous lining up by strangers]{.s-t1} 

[so that all are served on a \"first-come-first-served\"]{.s-t1} 

[basis.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Also, strangers often voluntarily share equally in]{.s-t1} 

[unexpected, unearned good and bad fortune. And,]{.s-t1} 

[as an obverse consequence of such \"fair-sharing\"]{.s-t1} 

[conduct, much reactive hostility occurs when fair]{.s-t1} 

[sharing is expected yet not provided.]{.s-t1} 

 

[It is interesting how the world\'s slavery was]{.s-t1} 

[pretty well abolished during the last three centuries]{.s-t1} 

[after being tolerated for a great many previous]{.s-t1} 

[centuries during which it coexisted with the world\'s]{.s-t1} 

[major religions. My guess is that Kantian Fairness]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency was a major contributor to this result.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Eight: Envy/Jealousy Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[A member of a species designed through evolutionary process to want often-scarce food is going]{.s-t1} 

[to be driven strongly toward getting food when it first sees food. And this is going to occur often and]{.s-t1} 

[tend to create some conflict when the food is seen]{.s-t1} 

[in the possession of another member of the same species. This is probably the evolutionary origin]{.s-t1} 

[of the envy/jealousy Tendency that lies so deep in]{.s-t1} 

[human nature.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Sibling jealousy is clearly very strong and usually greater in children than adults. It is often stronger than jealousy directed at strangers. Kantian]{.s-t1} 

[Fairness Tendency probably contributes to this]{.s-t1} 

[result.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Envy/jealousy is extreme in myth, religion,]{.s-t1} 

[and literature wherein, in account after account, it triggers hatred and injury. It was regarded as]{.s-t1} 

[so pernicious by the Jews of the civilization that preceded Christ that it was forbidden, by phrase]{.s-t1} 

[after phrase, in the laws of Moses. You were even]{.s-t1} 

[warned by the Prophet not to covet your neighbor\'s]{.s-t1} 

[donkey.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And envy/jealousy is also extreme in modern life. For instance, university communities often go]{.s-t1} 

[bananas when some university employee in money]{.s-t1} 

[management, or some professor in surgery, gets]{.s-t1} 

[annual compensation in multiples of the standard]{.s-t1} 

[professorial salary. And in modern investment]{.s-t1} 

[banks, law firms, etc., the envy/jealousy effects are]{.s-t1} 

[usually more extreme than they are in university]{.s-t1} 

[faculties. Many big law firms, fearing disorder from]{.s-t1} 

[envy/jealousy, have long treated all senior partners]{.s-t1} 

[alike in compensation, no matter how different]{.s-t1} 

[their contributions to firm welfare. As I have shared]{.s-t1} 

[the observation of life with Warren Buffett over decades, I have heard him wisely say on several occasions: \"It is not greed that drives the world, but]{.s-t1} 

[envy.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[And, because this is roughly right, one]{.s-t1} 

[would expect a vast coverage of envy/jealousy in]{.s-t1} 

[psychology textbooks. But no such vast coverage existed when I read my three textbooks. Indeed,]{.s-t1} 

[the very words \"envy\" and \"jealousy\" were often]{.s-t1} 

[absent from indexes.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Non Discussion of envy/jealousy is not a]{.s-t1} 

[phenomenon confined to psychology texts. When]{.s-t1} 

[did any of you last engage in any large group discussion of some issue wherein adult envy/jealousy was]{.s-t1} 

[identified as the cause of someone\'s argument?]{.s-t1} 

[There seems to be a general taboo against any such claim. If so, what accounts for the taboo?]{.s-t1} 

 

[My guess is that people widely and generally]{.s-t1} 

[sense that labeling some position as driven by envy/ jealousy will be regarded as extremely insulting]{.s-t1} 

[to the position taker, possibly more so when the diagnosis is correct than when it is wrong. And if calling a position \"envy-driven\" is perceived as]{.s-t1} 

[the equivalent of describing its holder as a childish mental basket case, then it is quite understandable]{.s-t1} 

[how a general taboo has arisen.]{.s-t1} 

 

[But should this general taboo extend to psychology texts when it creates such a large gap]{.s-t1} 

[in the correct, psychological explanation of what is]{.s-t1} 

[widespread and important? My answer is no.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Nine: Reciprocation Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[The automatic tendency of humans to reciprocate both favors and disfavors has long been noticed as extreme, as it is in apes, monkeys, dogs, and many less cognitively gifted animals. The tendency]{.s-t1} 

[clearly facilitates group cooperation for the benefit]{.s-t1} 

[of members. In this respect, it mimics much]{.s-t1} 

[genetic programming of the social insects.]{.s-t1} 

 

[We see the extreme power of the tendency]{.s-t1} 

[to reciprocate disfavors in some wars, wherein it increases hatred to a level causing very brutal]{.s-t1} 

[conduct. For long stretches in many wars, no]{.s-t1} 

[prisoners were taken; the only acceptable enemy]{.s-t1} 

[being a dead one. And sometimes that was not]{.s-t1} 

[enough, as in the case of Genghis Khan, who was]{.s-t1} 

[not satisfied with corpses. He insisted on their]{.s-t1} 

[being hacked into pieces.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One interesting mental exercise is to compare]{.s-t1} 

[Genghis Khan, who exercised extreme, lethal]{.s-t1} 

[hostility toward other men, with ants that display]{.s-t1} 

[extreme, lethal hostility toward members of their]{.s-t1} 

[own species that are not part of their breeding]{.s-t1} 

[colony. Genghis looks sweetly lovable when compared to the ants. The ants are more disposed]{.s-t1} 

[to fight and fight with more extreme cruelty.]{.s-t1} 

[Indeed, E. O. Wilson once waggishly suggested that]{.s-t1} 

[if ants were suddenly to get atom bombs, all ants]{.s-t1} 

[would be dead within eighteen hours. What both]{.s-t1} 

[human and ant history suggest is (1) that nature has]{.s-t1} 

[no general algorithm making intraspecies, turn-the]{.s-t1} 

[other-cheek behavior a booster of species survival, (2) that it is not clear that a country would have]{.s-t1} 

[good prospects were it to abandon all reciprocate disfavor tendency directed at outsiders, and (3) if]{.s-t1} 

[turn-the-other-cheek behavior is a good idea for a country as it deals with outsiders, man\'s culture is going to have to do a lot of heavy lifting because his]{.s-t1} 

[genes won\'t be of much help.]{.s-t1} 

 

[I next turn to man\'s reciprocated hostility that]{.s-t1} 

[falls well short of war. Peacetime hostility can be]{.s-t1} 

[pretty extreme, as in many modern cases of \"road]{.s-t1} 

[rage\" or injury-producing temper tantrums on]{.s-t1} 

[athletic fields.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The standard antidote to one\'s overactive]{.s-t1} 

[hostility is to train oneself to defer reaction. As my]{.s-t1} 

[smart friend Tom Murphy so frequently says, \"You can always tell the man off tomorrow if it is such a]{.s-t1} 

[good idea.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[Of course, the tendency to reciprocate favor for favor is also very intense, so much so that it occasionally reverses the course of reciprocated hostility.]{.s-t1} 

[Weird pauses in fighting have sometimes occurred]{.s-t1} 

[right in the middle of wars, triggered by some minor]{.s-t1} 

[courtesy or favor on the part of one side, followed]{.s-t1} 

[by favor reciprocation from the other side, and so]{.s-t1} 

[on, until fighting stopped for a considerable period.]{.s-t1} 

[This happened more than once in the trench]{.s-t1} 

[warfare of World War I, over big stretches of the front and much to the dismay of the generals.]{.s-t1} 

 

[It is obvious that commercial trade, a fundamental cause of modern prosperity. is enormously]{.s-t1} 

[facilitated by man\'s innate tendency to reciprocate favors. In trade, enlightened self-interest joining]{.s-t1} 

[with Reciprocation Tendency results in constructive conduct. Daily interchange in marriage is also]{.s-t1} 

[assisted by Reciprocation Tendency, without which]{.s-t1} 

[marriage would lose much of its allure.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And Reciprocation Tendency, insomuch as it causes good results, does not join forces only with the superpower of incentives. It also joins Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency in helping cause (1) the]{.s-t1} 

[fulfillment of promises made as part of a bargain,]{.s-t1} 

[including loyalty promises in marriage ceremonies.]{.s-t1} 

[and (2) correct behavior expected from persons]{.s-t1} 

[serving as priests, shoemakers, physicians, and all]{.s-t1} 

[else.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Like other psychological tendencies, and also]{.s-t1} 

[man\'s ability to turn somersaults, reciprocate-favor]{.s-t1} 

[tendency operates to a very considerable degree at a]{.s-t1} 

[subconscious level. This helps make the tendency]{.s-t1} 

[a strong force that can sometimes be used by some]{.s-t1} 

[men to mislead others, which happens all the time.]{.s-t1} 

 

[For instance, when an automobile salesman]{.s-t1} 

[graciously steers you into a comfortable place to sit and gives you a cup of coffee, you are very likely]{.s-t1} 

[being tricked, by this small courtesy alone, into]{.s-t1} 

[parting with an extra five hundred dollars. This is]{.s-t1} 

[far from the most extreme case of sales success that is rooted in a salesman dispensing minor favors.]{.s-t1} 

[However, in this scenario of buying a car, you are going to be disadvantaged by parting with an extra]{.s-t1} 

[five hundred dollars of your own money. This]{.s-t1} 

[potential loss will protect you to some extent.]{.s-t1} 

 

[But suppose you are the purchasing agent of someone else-a rich employer, for instance. Now]{.s-t1} 

[the minor favor you receive from the salesman]{.s-t1} 

[is less opposed by the threat of extra cost to you]{.s-t1} 

[because someone else is paying the extra cost.]{.s-t1} 

[Under such circumstances, the salesman is often]{.s-t1} 

[able to maximize his advantage, particularly when]{.s-t1} 

[government is the purchaser.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Wise employers, therefore, try to oppose reciprocate-favor tendencies of employees engaged in]{.s-t1} 

[purchasing. The simplest antidote works best:]{.s-t1} 

[Don\'t let them accept any favors from vendors.]{.s-t1} 

[Sam Walton agreed with this idea of absolute prohibition. He wouldn\'t let purchasing agents]{.s-t1} 

[accept so much as a hot dog from a vendor. Given]{.s-t1} 

[the subconscious level at which much Reciprocation Tendency operates, this policy of Walton\'s was profoundly correct. If I controlled the Defense]{.s-t1} 

[Department, its policies would mimic Walton\'s.]{.s-t1} 

 

[In a famous psychology experiment, Cialdini brilliantly demonstrated the power of \"compliance]{.s-t1} 

[practitioners\" to mislead people by triggering their]{.s-t1} 

[subconscious Reciprocation Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Carrying out this experiment, Cialdini caused]{.s-t1} 

[his \"compliance practitioners\" to wander around his]{.s-t1} 

[campus and ask strangers to supervise a bunch of juvenile delinquents on a trip to a zoo. Because this]{.s-t1} 

[happened on a campus, one person in six out of a large]{.s-t1} 

[sample actually agreed to do this. After accumulating this one-in-six statistic, Cialdini changed his procedure. His practitioners next wandered around]{.s-t1} 

[the campus asking strangers to devote a big chunk]{.s-t1} 

[of time every week for two years to the supervision of juvenile delinquents. This ridiculous request]{.s-t1} 

[got him a one hundred percent rejection rate. But the practitioner had a follow-up question: \"Will you at least spend one afternoon taking juvenile delinquents to a zoo?\" This raised Cialdini\'s former acceptance rate of l/6 to 1/2, a tripling.]{.s-t1} 

 

[What Cialdini\'s \"compliance practitioners\"]{.s-t1} 

[had done was make a small concession, which was]{.s-t1} 

[reciprocated by a small concession from the other side. This subconscious reciprocation of a concession by Cialdini\'s experimental subjects actually]{.s-t1} 

[caused a much increased percentage of them to end up irrationally agreeing to go to a zoo with juvenile]{.s-t1} 

[delinquents. Now, a professor who can invent an]{.s-t1} 

[experiment like that, which so powerfully demonstrates something so important, deserves much]{.s-t1} 

[recognition in the wider world, which he indeed]{.s-t1} 

[got to the credit of many universities that learned a]{.s-t1} 

[great deal from Cialdini.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Why is Reciprocation Tendency so important?]{.s-t1} 

[Well, consider the folly of having law students]{.s-t1} 

[graduate, and go out in the world representing]{.s-t1} 

[clients in negotiations, not knowing the nature]{.s-t1} 

[of the subconscious processes of the mind as]{.s-t1} 

[exhibited in Cialdini\'s experiment. Yet such folly]{.s-t1} 

[was prevalent in the law schools of the world for]{.s-t1} 

[decades, in fact, generations. The correct name for]{.s-t1} 

[that is educational malpractice. The law schools]{.s-t1} 

[didn\'t know, or care to teach, what Sam Walton so]{.s-t1} 

[well knew.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The importance and power of reciprocate-favor]{.s-t1} 

[tendency was also demonstrated in Cialdini\'s explanation of the foolish decision of the attorney general]{.s-t1} 

[of the United States to authorize the Watergate]{.s-t1} 

[burglary. There, an aggressive subordinate made]{.s-t1} 

[some extreme proposal for advancing Republican interests through use of some combination of whores and a gigantic yacht. When this ridiculous]{.s-t1} 

[request was rejected, the subordinate backed off,]{.s-t1} 

[in gracious concession, to merely asking for consent]{.s-t1} 

[to a burglary, and the attorney general went along.]{.s-t1} 

[Cialdini believes that subconscious Reciprocation Tendency thus became one important cause]{.s-t1} 

[of the resignation of a United States president in the Watergate debacle, and so do I. Reciprocation Tendency subtly causes many extreme and dangerous consequences, not just on rare occasions]{.s-t1} 

[but pretty much all the time.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Man\'s belief in reciprocate-favor tendency,]{.s-t1} 

[following eons of his practicing it, has done some]{.s-t1} 

[queer and bad things in religions. The ritualized]{.s-t1} 

[murder of the Phoenicians and the Aztecs, in which]{.s-t1} 

[they sacrificed human victims to their gods, was a]{.s-t1} 

[particularly egregious example. And we should not]{.s-t1} 

[forget that as late as the Punic Wars, the civilized]{.s-t1} 

[Romans, out of fear of defeat, returned in a few instances to the practice of human sacrifice. On the]{.s-t1} 

[other hand, the reciprocity-based, religion-boosting]{.s-t1} 

[idea of obtaining help from God in reciprocation]{.s-t1} 

[for good human behavior has probably been vastly]{.s-t1} 

[constructive.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Overall, both inside and outside religions, it]{.s-t1} 

[seems clear to me that Reciprocation Tendency\'s]{.s-t1} 

[constructive contributions to man far outweigh]{.s-t1} 

[its destructive effects. In cases of psychological]{.s-t1} 

[tendencies being used to counter or prevent bad]{.s-t1} 

[results from one or more other psychological]{.s-t1} 

[tendencies-for instance, in the case of interventions to end chemical dependency-you will usually]{.s-t1} 

[find Reciprocation Tendency performing strongly]{.s-t1} 

[on the constructive side.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And the very best part of human life probably]{.s-t1} 

[lies in relationships of affection wherein parties are more interested in pleasing than being pleased-a]{.s-t1} 

[not uncommon outcome in display of reciprocate]{.s-t1} 

[favor tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Before we leave reciprocate-favor tendency,]{.s-t1} 

[the final phenomenon we will consider is widespread human misery from feelings of guilt. To the]{.s-t1} 

[extent the feeling of guilt has an evolutionary base, I believe the most plausible cause is the mental]{.s-t1} 

[conflict triggered in one direction by reciprocate]{.s-t1} 

[favor tendency and in the opposite direction by]{.s-t1} 

[reward super response tendency pushing one to enjoy one hundred percent of some good thing. Of]{.s-t1} 

[course, human culture has often greatly boosted]{.s-t1} 

[the genetic tendency to suffer from feelings of]{.s-t1} 

[guilt. Most especially, religious culture has imposed]{.s-t1} 

[hard-to-follow ethical and devotional demands on]{.s-t1} 

[people. There is a charming Irish Catholic priest in]{.s-t1} 

[my neighborhood who, with rough accuracy, often says, \"The old Jews may have invented guilt, but we Catholics perfected it.\" And if you, like me and this priest, believe that, averaged out, feelings of guilt do more good than harm, you may join in my]{.s-t1} 

[special gratitude for reciprocate-favor tendency, no matter how unpleasant you find feelings of guilt.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Ten: Influence -from-Mere-association Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[In the standard conditioned reflexes studied by]{.s-t1} 

[Skinner and most common in the world, responsive]{.s-t1} 

[behavior, creating a new habit, is directly triggered]{.s-t1} 

[by rewards previously bestowed. For instance, a]{.s-t1} 

[man buys a can of branded shoe polish, has a good]{.s-t1} 

[experience with it when shining his shoes, and]{.s-t1} 

[because of this \"reward,\" buys the same shoe polish]{.s-t1} 

[when he needs another can.]{.s-t1} 

 

[But there is another type of conditioned reflex]{.s-t1} 

[wherein mere association triggers a response. For]{.s-t1} 

[instance, consider the case of many men who]{.s-t1} 

[have been trained by their previous experience in]{.s-t1} 

[life to believe that when several similar items are presented for purchase, the one with the highest price will have the highest quality. Knowing this, some seller of an ordinary industrial product will often change his product\'s trade dress and raise]{.s-t1} 

[its price significantly hoping that quality-seeking buyers will be tricked into becoming purchasers by]{.s-t1} 

[mere association of his product and its high price.]{.s-t1} 

 

[This industrial practice frequently is effective in]{.s-t1} 

[driving up sales and even more so in driving up profits. For instance, it worked wonderfully with high-priced power tools for a long time. And it would work better yet with high-priced pumps at]{.s-t1} 

[the bottom of oil wells. With luxury goods, the]{.s-t1} 

[process works with a special boost because buyers]{.s-t1} 

[who pay high prices often gain extra status from]{.s-t1} 

[thus demonstrating both their good taste and their]{.s-t1} 

[ability to pay.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Even association that appears to be trivial, if]{.s-t1} 

[carefully planned, can have extreme and peculiar]{.s-t1} 

[effects on purchasers of products. The target]{.s-t1} 

[purchaser of shoe polish may like pretty girls. And]{.s-t1} 

[so he chooses the polish with the pretty girl on the can or the one with the pretty girl in the last ad for]{.s-t1} 

[shoe polish that he saw]{.s-t1} 

 

[Advertisers know about the power of mere association. You won\'t see Coke advertised alongside]{.s-t1} 

[some account of the death of a child. Instead. Coke ads picture life as happier than reality.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Similarly, it is not from mere chance that military bands play such impressive music. That kind of music, appearing in mere association with military service, helps to attract soldiers and keep]{.s-t1} 

[them in the army. Most armies have learned to use]{.s-t1} 

[mere association in this successful way.]{.s-t1} 

 

[However, the most damaging miscalculations]{.s-t1} 

[from mere association do not ordinarily come from]{.s-t1} 

[advertisers and music providers.]{.s-t1} 

[Some of the most important miscalculations]{.s-t1} 

[come from what is accidentally associated with]{.s-t1} 

[one\'s past success, or one\'s liking and loving, or]{.s-t1} 

[one\'s disliking and hating, which includes a natural]{.s-t1} 

[hatred for bad news.]{.s-t1} 

 

[To avoid being misled by the mere association]{.s-t1} 

[of some fact with past success, use this memory]{.s-t1} 

[clue. Think of Napoleon and Hitler when they]{.s-t1} 

[invaded Russia after using their armies with]{.s-t1} 

[much success elsewhere. And there are plenty]{.s-t1} 

[of mundane examples of results like those of Napoleon and Hitler. For instance, a man foolishly]{.s-t1} 

[gambles in a casino and yet wins. This unlikely]{.s-t1} 

[correlation causes him to try the casino again, or]{.s-t1} 

[again and again, to his horrid detriment. Or a man]{.s-t1} 

[gets lucky in an odds-against venture headed by an untalented friend. So influenced, he tries again what worked before-with terrible results.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The proper antidotes to being made such a]{.s-t1} 

[patsy by past success are (1) to carefully examine]{.s-t1} 

[each past success, looking for accidental, non]{.s-t1} 

[causative factors associated with such success that will tend to mislead as one appraises odds implicit]{.s-t1} 

[in a proposed new undertaking and (2) to look for]{.s-t1} 

[dangerous aspects of the new undertaking that were]{.s-t1} 

[not present when past success occurred.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The damage to the mind that can come from]{.s-t1} 

[liking and loving was once demonstrated by]{.s-t1} 

[obviously false testimony given by an otherwise very admirable woman, the wife of a party in a jury]{.s-t1} 

[case. The famous opposing counsel wanted to]{.s-t1} 

[minimize his attack on such an admirable woman]{.s-t1} 

[yet destroy the credibility of her testimony. And so,]{.s-t1} 

[in his closing argument, he came to her testimony]{.s-t1} 

[last. He then shook his head sadly and said, \"What]{.s-t1} 

[are we to make of such testimony? The answer lies]{.s-t1} 

[in the old rhyme:]{.s-t1} 

 

[\'As the husband is,]{.s-t1} 

[So the wife is.]{.s-t1} 

[She is married to a clown,]{.s-t1} 

[And the grossness of his nature]{.s-t1} 

[Drags her down.\"\']{.s-t1} 

 

[The jury disbelieved the woman\'s testimony.]{.s-t1} 

[They easily recognized the strong mis influence of]{.s-t1} 

[love on her cognition. And we now often see even]{.s-t1} 

[stronger mis influence from love as tearful mothers,]{.s-t1} 

[with heartfelt conviction, declare before TV cameras]{.s-t1} 

[the innocence of their obviously guilty sons.]{.s-t1} 

 

[People disagree about how much blindness]{.s-t1} 

[should accompany the association called love. In]{.s-t1} 

[Poor Richard\'s Almanac Franklin counseled: \"Keep]{.s-t1} 

[your eyes wide open before marriage and half shut]{.s-t1} 

[thereafter.\" Perhaps this \"eyes-half-shut\" solution]{.s-t1} 

[is about right, but I favor a tougher prescription: \"See it like it is and love anyway.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[Hating and disliking also cause miscalculation triggered by mere association. In business,]{.s-t1} 

[I commonly see people under appraise both the]{.s-t1} 

[competency and morals of competitors they dislike.]{.s-t1} 

[This is a dangerous practice, usually disguised]{.s-t1} 

[because it occurs on a subconscious basis.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Another common bad effect from the mere association of a person and a hated outcome is displayed]{.s-t1} 

[in \"Persian Messenger Syndrome.\" Ancient]{.s-t1} 

[Persians actually killed some messengers whose]{.s-t1} 

[sole fault was that they brought home truthful bad news, say, of a battle lost. It was actually safer for]{.s-t1} 

[the messenger to run away and hide, instead of doing his job as a wiser boss would have wanted it]{.s-t1} 

[done.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And Persian Messenger Syndrome is alive and]{.s-t1} 

[well in modern life, albeit in less lethal versions. It is actually dangerous in many careers co be a]{.s-t1} 

[carrier of unwelcome news. Union negotiators]{.s-t1} 

[and employer representatives often know this, and it leads to many tragedies in labor relations.]{.s-t1} 

[Sometimes lawyers, knowing their clients will]{.s-t1} 

[hate them if they recommend an unwelcome but]{.s-t1} 

[wise settlement, ]{.s-t1}[w]{.s-t7}[ill carry on to disaster. Even]{.s-t1} 

[in places well known for high cognition, one will]{.s-t1} 

[sometimes find Persian Messenger Syndrome.]{.s-t1} 

[For instance, years ago, two major oil companies]{.s-t1} 

[litigated in a Texas trial court over some ambiguity]{.s-t1} 

[in an operating agreement covering one of the]{.s-t1} 

[largest oil reservoirs in the Western hemisphere.]{.s-t1} 

[My guess is that the cause of the trial was some general counsel\'s unwillingness to carry bad news to]{.s-t1} 

[a strong-minded CEO.]{.s-t1} 

 

[CBS, in its late heyday, was famous for occurrence of Persian Messenger Syndrome because]{.s-t1} 

[Chairman Paley was hostile to people who brought him bad news. The result was that Paley lived in]{.s-t1} 

[a cocoon of unreality, from which he made one bad]{.s-t1} 

[deal after another, even exchanging a large share of]{.s-t1} 

[CBS for a company that had to be liquidated shortly]{.s-t1} 

[thereafter.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The proper antidote to creating Persian]{.s-t1} 

[Messenger Syndrome and its bad effects, like those]{.s-t1} 

[at CBS, is to develop, through exercise of will, a]{.s-t1} 

[habit of welcoming bad news. At Berkshire, there is]{.s-t1} 

[a common injunction: \"Always tell us the bad news promptly. It is only the good news that can wait.\" It also helps to be so wise and informed that people]{.s-t1} 

[fear not telling you bad news because you are so]{.s-t1} 

[likely to get it elsewhere.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Influence-from-Mere-Association Tendency]{.s-t1} 

[often has a shocking effect that helps swamp]{.s-t1} 

[the normal tendency to return favor for favor.]{.s-t1} 

[Sometimes, when one receives a favor, his condition]{.s-t1} 

[is unpleasant, due to poverty, sickness, subjugation, or something else. In addition, the favor may]{.s-t1} 

[trigger an envy-driven dislike for the person who]{.s-t1} 

[was in so favorable a state that he could easily be]{.s-t1} 

[a favor giver. Under such circumstances, the favor]{.s-t1} 

[receiver, prompted partly by mere association of the favor giver with past pain, will not only dislike]{.s-t1} 

[the man who helped him but also try to injure him.]{.s-t1} 

[This accounts for a famous response, sometimes]{.s-t1} 

[dubiously attributed to Henry Ford: \"Why does that man hate me so? I never did anything for him.\" I have a friend, whom I will now call \"Glotz,\" who]{.s-t1} 

[had an amusing experience in favor-giving. Glotz]{.s-t1} 

[owned an apartment building that he had bought]{.s-t1} 

[because he wanted, eventually, to use the land in]{.s-t1} 

[different development. Pending this outcome,]{.s-t1} 

[Glotz was very lenient in collecting below-market]{.s-t1} 

[rents from tenants. When, at last, there was a public hearing on Glotz\'s proposal to tear down the]{.s-t1} 

[building, one tenant who was far behind in his rent]{.s-t1} 

[payments was particularly angry and hostile. He came to the public hearing and said, \"This proposal]{.s-t1} 

[is outrageous. Glotz doesn\'t need any more money. I know this because I was supported in college by]{.s-t1} 

[Glotz fellowships.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[A final serious clump of bad thinking caused by]{.s-t1} 

[mere association lies in the common use of classification stereotypes. Because Pete knows that Joe is ninety years old and that most ninety-year-old persons don\'t think very well, Pete appraises old Joe as a thinking klutz even if old Joe still thinks very well. Or, because Jane is a white-haired woman, and Pete knows no old women good at higher math, Pete appraises Jane as no good at it even if Jane is a whiz. This sort of wrong thinking is both natural]{.s-t1} 

[and common. Pete\'s antidote is not to believe that,]{.s-t1} 

[on average, ninety-year-olds think as well as forty]{.s-t1} 

[year-olds or that there are as many females as males among Ph. D.\'s in math. Instead, just as he must]{.s-t1} 

[learn that trend does not always correctly predict destiny, he must learn that the average dimension in some group will not reliably guide him to the dimension of some specific item. Otherwise Pete will make many errors, like that of the fellow who drowned in a river that averaged out only eighteen]{.s-t1} 

[inches deep.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Eleven: Simple, Pain-Avoiding Psychological Denial]{.s-t4} 

 

[487]{.s-t1} 

 

[This phenomenon first hit me hard in World War II when the super athlete, superstudent son]{.s-t1} 

[of a family friend flew off over the Atlantic Ocean]{.s-t1} 

[and never came back. His mother, who was a very]{.s-t1} 

[sane woman, then refused to believe he was dead.]{.s-t1} 

[That\'s Simple, Pain-Avoiding Psychological Denial. The reality is too painful to bear, so one distorts the facts until they become bearable. We all do that to some extent, often causing terrible problems. The]{.s-t1} 

[tendency's most extreme outcomes are usually mixed]{.s-t1} 

[up with love, death, and chemical dependency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Where denial is used to make dying easier, the conduct meets almost no criticism. Who would]{.s-t1} 

[begrudge a fellow man such help at such a time? But some people hope to leave life hewing to the iron prescription, \"It is not necessary to hope]{.s-t1} 

[in order to persevere.\" And there is something]{.s-t1} 

[admirable in anyone able to do this.]{.s-t1} 

 

[In chemical dependency, wherein morals]{.s-t1} 

[usually break down horribly, addicted persons tend]{.s-t1} 

[to believe that they remain in respectable condition, with respectable prospects. They thus display an extremely unrealistic denial of reality as they go deeper and deeper into deterioration. In my]{.s-t1} 

[youth, Freudian remedies failed utterly in reversing]{.s-t1} 

[chemical dependency, but nowadays Alcoholics Anonymous routinely achieves a fifty percent cure]{.s-t1} 

[rate by causing several psychological tendencies]{.s-t1} 

[to act together to counter addiction. However, the cure process is typically difficult and draining, and a fifty percent success rate implies a fifty percent failure rate. One should stay far away from any conduct at all likely to drift into chemical dependency. Even a small chance of suffering so great a]{.s-t1} 

[damage should be avoided.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Twelve: Excessive Self-Regard Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[We all commonly observe the excessive self]{.s-t1} 

[regard of man. He mostly misappraises himself on]{.s-t1} 

[the high side, like the ninety percent of Swedish drivers that judge themselves to be above average.]{.s-t1} 

[Such mis appraisals also apply to a person\'s major]{.s-t1} 

[\"possessions.\" One spouse usually over appraises]{.s-t1} 

[the other spouse. And a man\'s children are likewise appraised higher by him than they are likely to be in a more objective view. Even man\'s minor]{.s-t1} 

[possessions tend to be over appraised. Once owned,]{.s-t1} 

[they suddenly become worth more to him than he]{.s-t1} 

[would pay if they were offered for sale to him and he didn\'t already own them. There is a name in psychology for this over appraise-your-own-possessions phenomenon: the \"endowment effect.\" And]{.s-t1} 

[all man\'s decisions are suddenly regarded by him as better than would have been the case lust before he made them.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Man\'s excess of self-regard typically makes him strongly prefer people like himself. Psychology]{.s-t1} 

[professors have had much fun demonstrating this effect in \"lost-wallet\" experiments. Their]{.s-t1} 

[experiments all show that the finder of a lost wallet containing identity clues will be most likely to]{.s-t1} 

[return the wallet when the owner most closely]{.s-t1} 

[resembles the finder. Given this quality in psychological nature, cliquish groups of similar persons will always be a very influential part of human culture,]{.s-t1} 

[even after we wisely try to dampen the worst]{.s-t1} 

[effects.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Some of the worst consequences in modern life come when dysfunctional groups of cliquish]{.s-t1} 

[persons, dominated by Excessive Self-Regard]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency, select as new members of their organizations persons who are very much like themselves. Thus if the English department at an elite university becomes mentally dysfunctional or the sales]{.s-t1} 

[department of a brokerage firm slips into routine fraud, the problem will have a natural tendency]{.s-t1} 

[to get worse and to be quite resistant to change for the better. So also with a police department]{.s-t1} 

[or prison-guard unit or political group gone sour]{.s-t1} 

[and countless other places mired in evil and folly, such as the worst of our big-city teachers\' unions that harm our children by preventing discharge of ineffective teachers. Therefore, some of the most]{.s-t1} 

[useful members of our civilization are those who are willing to \"clean house\" when they find a mess]{.s-t1} 

[under their ambit of control.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Well, naturally, all forms of excess of self-regard cause much error. How could it be otherwise?]{.s-t1} 

[Let us consider some foolish gambling]{.s-t1} 

[decisions. In lotteries, the play is much lower when]{.s-t1} 

[numbers are distributed randomly than it is when]{.s-t1} 

[the player picks his own number. This is quite irrational. The odds are almost exactly the same and much against the player. Because state lotteries]{.s-t1} 

[take advantage of man\'s irrational love of self picked numbers, modern man buys more lottery]{.s-t1} 

[tickets than he otherwise would have. with each]{.s-t1} 

[purchase foolish.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Intensify man\'s love of his own conclusions by]{.s-t1} 

[adding the possessory wallop from the \"endowment effect,\" and you will find that a man who has already]{.s-t1} 

[bought a pork-belly future on a commodity exchange]{.s-t1} 

[now foolishly believes, even more strongly than]{.s-t1} 

[before, in the merits of his speculative bet.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And foolish sports betting, by people who love sports and think they know a lot about relative]{.s-t1} 

[merits of teams, is a lot more addictive than race track betting-partly because of man\'s automatic]{.s-t1} 

[Over appraisal of his own complicated conclusions.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Also extremely counterproductive is man\'s]{.s-t1} 

[tendency to bet, time after time, in games of skill,]{.s-t1} 

[like golf or poker, against people who are obviously]{.s-t1} 

[much better players. Excessive Self-Regard]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency diminishes the foolish bettor\'s accuracy]{.s-t1} 

[in appraising his relative degree of talent.]{.s-t1} 

 

[More counterproductive yet are man\'s appraisals, typically excessive, of the quality of the]{.s-t1} 

[future service he is to provide to his business. His over appraisal of these prospective contributions will]{.s-t1} 

[frequently cause disaster.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Excesses of self-regard often cause bad hiring]{.s-t1} 

[decisions because employers grossly over appraise]{.s-t1} 

[the worth of their own conclusions that rely on impressions in face-to-face contact. The correct]{.s-t1} 

[antidote to this sort of folly is to underweigh face]{.s-t1} 

[to-face impressions and overweigh the applicant\'s]{.s-t1} 

[past record.]{.s-t1} 

 

[I once chose exactly this course of action]{.s-t1} 

[while I served as chairman of an academic search committee. I convinced fellow committee]{.s-t1} 

[members to stop all further interviews and simply]{.s-t1} 

[appoint a person whose achievement record was]{.s-t1} 

[much better than that of any other applicant. And when it was suggested to me that I wasn\'t giving \"academic due process,\" I replied that I was the one being true to academic values because I was using academic research showing poor predictive value of]{.s-t1} 

[impressions from face-to-face interviews.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Because man is likely to be over influenced by]{.s-t1} 

[face-to-face impressions that by definition involve his active participation, a job candidate who is a]{.s-t1} 

[marvelous \"presenter\" often causes great danger under modern executive-search practice. In my opinion, Hewlett-Packard faced just such a danger when it interviewed the articulate, dynamic Carly Fiorina in its search for a new CEO. And I believe]{.s-t1} 

[(1) that Hewlett-Packard made a bad decision when]{.s-t1} 

[it chose Ms. Fiorina and Q) that this bad decision would not have been made if Hewlett-Packard had]{.s-t1} 

[taken the methodological precautions it would have taken if it knew more psychology.]{.s-t1} 

 

[There is a famous passage somewhere in]{.s-t1} 

[Tolstoy that illuminates the power of Excessive]{.s-t1} 

[Self-Regard Tendency. According to Tolstoy, the]{.s-t1} 

[worst criminals don\'t appraise themselves as all that bad. They come to believe either (1) that they didn\'t commit their crimes or (2) that, considering the pressures and disadvantages of their lives, it is]{.s-t1} 

[understandable and forgivable that they behaved as]{.s-t1} 

[they did and became what they became.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The second half of the \"Tolstoy effect\", where the man makes excuses for his fixable poor performance, instead of providing the fix, is enormously important. Because a majority of mankind will try]{.s-t1} 

[to get along by making way too many unreasonable excuses for fixable poor performance, it is very important to have personal and institutional antidotes limiting the ravages of such folly. On the personal level a man should try to face the two simple facts: (1) fixable but unfixed bad performance is bad character and tends to create more]{.s-t1} 

[of itself, causing more damage to the excuse giver with each tolerated instance, and (2) in demanding places, like athletic teams and General Electric,]{.s-t1} 

[you are almost sure to be discarded in due course if you keep giving excuses instead of behaving as you should. The main institutional antidotes to this part of the \"Tolstoy effect\" are (1) a fair, meritocratic, demanding culture plus personnel handling]{.s-t1} 

[methods that build up morale and (2) severance]{.s-t1} 

[of the worst offenders. Of course, when you can\'t]{.s-t1} 

[sever, as in the case of your own child, you must try to fix the child as best you can. I once heard of]{.s-t1} 

[a child-teaching method so effective that the child remembered the learning experience over fifty years later. The child later became Dean of the]{.s-t1} 

[USC School of Music and then related to me what]{.s-t1} 

[his father said when he saw his child taking candy]{.s-t1} 

[from the stock of his employer with the excuse that]{.s-t1} 

[he intended to replace it later. The father said, \"Son, it would be better for you to simply take all you want]{.s-t1} 

[and call yourself a thief every time you do it\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[The best antidote to folly from an excess of]{.s-t1} 

[self-regard is to force yourself to be more objective]{.s-t1} 

[when you are thinking about yourself, your family]{.s-t1} 

[and friends, your property, and the value of your past and future activity. This isn\'t easy to do well]{.s-t1} 

[and won\'t work perfectly, but it will work much]{.s-t1} 

[better than simply letting psychological nature take]{.s-t1} 

[its normal course.]{.s-t1} 

 

[While an excess of self-regard is often counter]{.s-t1} 

[productive in its effects on cognition, or can cause]{.s-t1} 

[some weird successes from overconfidence that]{.s-t1} 

[happens to cause success. This factor accounts for the adage: \"Never underestimate the man who]{.s-t1} 

[overestimates himself.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[Of course, some high self-appraisals are correct]{.s-t1} 

[and serve better than false modesty. Moreover, self-regard in the form of a justified pride in a job well done, or a life well lived, is a large constructive force. Without such justified pride, many]{.s-t1} 

[more airplanes would crash. \"Pride\" is another]{.s-t1} 

[word generally left out of psychology textbooks,]{.s-t1} 

[and this omission is not a good idea. It is also not a]{.s-t1} 

[good idea to construe the bible\'s parable about the]{.s-t1} 

[Pharisee and the Publican as condemning all pride.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Of all forms of useful pride, perhaps the most desirable is a justified pride in being trustworthy.]{.s-t1} 

[Moreover, the trustworthy man, even after allowing]{.s-t1} 

[for the inconveniences of his chosen course, ordinarily has a life that averages our better than he would have if he provided less reliability.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Thirteen: Overoptimism Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[About three centuries before the birth of Christ,]{.s-t1} 

[Demosthenes, the most famous Greek orator, said. \"What a man wishes, that also will he believe.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[Demosthenes, parsed out, was thus saying]{.s-t1} 

[that man displays not only Simple, Pain-Avoiding]{.s-t1} 

[Psychological Denial but also an excess of optimism]{.s-t1} 

[even when he is already doing well.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The Greek orator was clearly right about]{.s-t1} 

[an excess of optimism being the normal human]{.s-t1} 

[condition, even when pain or the threat of pain]{.s-t1} 

[is absent. Witness happy people buying lottery]{.s-t1} 

[tickets or believing that credit-furnishing, delivery]{.s-t1} 

[making grocery stores were going to displace a great]{.s-t1} 

[many superefficient cash-and-carry supermarkets.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One standard antidote to foolish optimism is]{.s-t1} 

[trained, habitual use of the simple probability math]{.s-t1} 

[of Fermat and Pascal, taught in my youth to high]{.s-t1} 

[school sophomores. The mental rules of thumb]{.s-t1} 

[that evolution gives you to deal with risk are not adequate. They resemble the dysfunctional golf]{.s-t1} 

[grip you would have if you relied on a grip driven]{.s-t1} 

[by evolution instead of golf lessons.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Fourteen: Deprival- Super Reaction Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[The quantity of man\'s pleasure from a ten dollar gain does not exactly match the quantity of]{.s-t1} 

[his displeasure from a ten-dollar loss. That is, the]{.s-t1} 

[loss seems to hurt much more than the gain seems]{.s-t1} 

[to help. Moreover, if a man almost gets something he greatly wants and has it jerked away from him at the last moment, he will react much as if he had long owned the reward and had it jerked away. I include the natural human reactions to both kinds]{.s-t1} 

[of loss experience-the loss of the possessed reward and the loss of the almost-possessed reward-under]{.s-t1} 

[one description, Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[In displaying Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency,]{.s-t1} 

[man frequently incurs disadvantage by misframing his problems. He will often compare what is near]{.s-t1} 

[instead of what really matters. For instance, a man with \$10 million in his brokerage account will often be extremely irritated by the accidental loss of \$100 out of the \$300 in his wallet.]{.s-t1} 

 

[The Mungers once owned a tame and good natured dog that displayed the canine version of]{.s-t1} 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency. There was only]{.s-t1} 

[one way to get bitten by this dog. And that was]{.s-t1} 

[to try and take some food away from him after he already had it in his mouth. If you did that, this friendly dog would automatically bite. He couldn\'t help it. Nothing could be more stupid than for the dog to bite his master. But the dog couldn\'t]{.s-t1} 

[help being foolish. He had an automatic Deprival]{.s-t1} 

[Super Reaction Tendency in his nature.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Humans are much the same as this Munger dog.]{.s-t1} 

[A man ordinarily reacts with irrational intensity to]{.s-t1} 

[even a small loss, or threatened loss, of property,]{.s-t1} 

[love, friendship, dominated territory, opportunity,]{.s-t1} 

[status, or any other valued thing. As a natural]{.s-t1} 

[result, bureaucratic infighting over the threatened]{.s-t1} 

[loss of dominated territory often causes immense damage to an institution as a whole. This factor, among others, accounts for much of the wisdom of Jack Welch\'s long fight against bureaucratic ills at General Electric. Few business leaders have ever]{.s-t1} 

[conducted wiser campaigns.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency often]{.s-t1} 

[protects ideological or religious views by triggering]{.s-t1} 

[dislike and hatred directed toward vocal nonbelievers. This happens, in part, because the ideas of the nonbelievers, if they spread, will diminish]{.s-t1} 

[the influence of views that are now supported]{.s-t1} 

[by a comfortable environment including a strong]{.s-t1} 

[belief-maintenance system. University Liberal]{.s-t1} 

[arts departments, law schools, and business organizations all display plenty of such ideology-based group think that rejects almost all conflicting inputs.]{.s-t1} 

[When the vocal critic is a former believer, hostility]{.s-t1} 

[is often boosted both by (1) a concept of betrayal]{.s-t1} 

[that triggers additional Deprival-Super Reaction]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency because a colleague is lost and (2) fears that conflicting views will have extra persuasive]{.s-t1} 

[power when they come from a former colleague. The foregoing considerations help account for the old idea of heresy, which for centuries justified]{.s-t1} 

[much killing of heretics, frequently after torture]{.s-t1} 

[and frequently accomplished by burning the victim]{.s-t1} 

[alive.]{.s-t1} 

 

[It is almost everywhere the case that extremes]{.s-t1} 

[of ideology are maintained with great intensity]{.s-t1} 

[and with great antipathy to non-believers, causing]{.s-t1} 

[extremes of cognitive dysfunction. This happens, I believe, because two psychological tendencies are usually acting concurrently toward this same sad result: ( I ) Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency, plus (2) Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[One antidote to intense, deliberate maintenance of group think is an extreme culture of courtesy, kept in place despite ideological differences, like the behavior of the justices now serving on the U.S. Supreme Court. Another antidote is to]{.s-t1} 

[deliberately bring in able and articulate disbelievers of incumbent group think. Successful corrective]{.s-t1} 

[measures to evil examples of group think maintenance have included actions like that of Derek Bok]{.s-t1} 

[when, as president of Harvard, he started disapproving tenure appointments proposed by ideologues at Harvard Law School.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Even a one-degree loss from a 1SO-degree view will sometime create enough Deprival-Super reaction Tendency to turn a neighbor into an enemy, as I once observed when I bought a house from one]{.s-t1} 

[of two neighbors locked into hatred by a tiny tree]{.s-t1} 

[newly installed by one of them.]{.s-t1} 

 

[As the case of these two neighbors illustrated,]{.s-t1} 

[the clamor of almost any group of neighbors]{.s-t1} 

[displaying irrational, extreme deprival-super reaction over some trifle in a zoning hearing is not a pretty thing to watch. Such bad behavior drives some people from the zoning field. I once bought]{.s-t1} 

[some golf clubs from an artisan who was formerly a lawyer. When I asked him what kind of law he had practiced, I expected to hear him say, \"divorce law.\"]{.s-t1} 

[But his answer was, \"zoning law.\"]{.s-t1} 

 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency has ghastly]{.s-t1} 

[effects in labor relations. Most of the deaths in the labor strife that occurred before World War I came]{.s-t1} 

[when employers tried to reduce wages. Nowadays,]{.s-t1} 

[we see fewer deaths and more occasions when]{.s-t1} 

[whole companies disappear. as competition requires either takeaways from labor-which it will not consent to-or death of the business. Deprival]{.s-t1} 

[Super Reaction Tendency causes much of this labor]{.s-t1} 

[resistance, often in cases where it would be in]{.s-t1} 

[labor\'s interest to make a different decision.]{.s-t1} 

 

[In contexts other than labor relations, takeaways are also difficult to get. Many tragedies, therefore,]{.s-t1} 

[occur that would have been avoided had there been]{.s-t1} 

[more rationality and less subconscious heed of the]{.s-t1} 

[imperative from Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency is also a huge]{.s-t1} 

[contributor to ruin from compulsion to gamble.]{.s-t1} 

[First, it causes the gambler to have a passion to]{.s-t1} 

[get even once he has suffered loss, and the passion]{.s-t1} 

[grows with the loss. Second, the most addictive]{.s-t1} 

[forms of gambling provide a lot of near misses and]{.s-t1} 

[each one triggers Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency.]{.s-t1} 

[Some slot machine creators are vicious in exploiting]{.s-t1} 

[this weakness of man. Electronic machines enable]{.s-t1} 

[these creators to produce a lot of meaningless bar]{.s-t1} 

[bar-lemon results that greatly increase play by fools]{.s-t1} 

[who think they have very nearly won large rewards.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency often does]{.s-t1} 

[much damage to man in open-outcry auctions. The \"social proof\' that we will next consider tends]{.s-t1} 

[to convince man that the last price from another]{.s-t1} 

[bidder was reasonable, and then Deprival-Super]{.s-t1} 

[reaction Tendency prompts him strongly to top the last bid. The best antidote to being thus triggered]{.s-t1} 

[into paying foolish prices at open-outcry auctions]{.s-t1} 

[is the simple Buffett practice: Don\'t go to such]{.s-t1} 

[auctions.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency and Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency often join to cause one form of business failure. In this form of ruin,]{.s-t1} 

[a man gradually uses up all his good assets in a]{.s-t1} 

[fruitless attempt to rescue a big venture going bad.]{.s-t1} 

[One of the best antidotes to this folly is good poker]{.s-t1} 

[skill learned young. The teaching value of poker]{.s-t1} 

[demonstrates that not all effective teaching occurs on a standard academic path.]{.s-t1} 

 

[I myself, the would-be instructor here, many]{.s-t1} 

[decades ago made a big mistake caused in part by]{.s-t1} 

[subconscious operation of my Deprival-Super reaction Tendency. A friendly broker called and offered]{.s-t1} 

[me 300 shares of ridiculously underpriced, very thinly traded Belridge Oil at \$115 per share, which I purchased using cash I had on hand. The next day,]{.s-t1} 

[he offered me 1,500 more shares at the same price, which I declined to buy partly because I could only have made the purchase had I sold something or borrowed the required \$173,000. This was a very irrational decision. I was a well-to-do man with]{.s-t1} 

[no debt; there was no risk of loss; and similar no risk opportunities were not likely to come along.]{.s-t1} 

[Within two years, Belridge Oil sold out to Shell at a price of about \$3,700 per share, which made me about \$5.4 million poorer than I would have been had I then been psychologically acute. As this tale]{.s-t1} 

[demonstrates, psychological ignorance can be very]{.s-t1} 

[expensive.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Some people may question my defining]{.s-t1} 

[Deprival-Super Reaction Tendency to include reaction to profit barely missed, as in the well]{.s-t1} 

[documented responses of slot machine players. However, I believe that I haven\'t defined the tendency as broadly as I should. My reason for]{.s-t1} 

[suggesting an even broader definition is that many Berkshire Hathaway shareholders I know never sell]{.s-t1} 

[or give away a single share after immense gains in]{.s-t1} 

[market value have occurred. Some of this reaction]{.s-t1} 

[is caused by rational calculation, and some is, no]{.s-t1} 

[doubt, attributable to some combination of (1) reward super response, (2) \"status quo bias\" from Inconsistency-Avoidance Tendency, and (3) \"The]{.s-t1} 

[endowment effect\" from Excessive Self-Regard Tendency. But I believe the single strongest]{.s-t1} 

[irrational explanation is a form of Deprival-Super]{.s-t1} 

[reaction Tendency. Many of these shareholders]{.s-t1} 

[simply can\'t stand the idea of having their Berkshire]{.s-t1} 

[Hathaway holdings smaller. Partly they dislike]{.s-t1} 

[facing what they consider an impairment of identity,]{.s-t1} 

[but mostly they fear missing out on future gains]{.s-t1} 

[from stock sold or given away.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Fifteen: Social-Proof Tendency]{.s-t4} 

 

[The otherwise complex behavior of man is]{.s-t1} 

[much simplified when he automatically thinks and]{.s-t1} 

[does what he observes to be thought and done around him. And such followership often works fine. For instance, what simpler way could there be to find out how to walk to a big football game in a]{.s-t1} 

[strange city than by following the flow of the crowd.]{.s-t1} 

[For some such reason, man\'s evolution left him with]{.s-t1} 

[Social-Proof Tendency, an automatic tendency to think and act as he sees others around him thinking]{.s-t1} 

[and acting.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Psychology professors love Social-Proof]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency because in their experiments it causes]{.s-t1} 

[ridiculous results. For instance, if a professor]{.s-t1} 

[arranges for some stranger to enter an elevator]{.s-t1} 

[wherein ten \"compliance practitioners\" are all]{.s-t1} 

[silently standing so that they face the rear of the]{.s-t1} 

[elevator, the stranger will often turn around and do]{.s-t1} 

[the same. The psychology professors can also use]{.s-t1} 

[Social-Proof Tendency to cause people to make]{.s-t1} 

[large and ridiculous measurement errors.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And, of course, teenagers\' parents usually]{.s-t1} 

[learn more than they would like about teenagers\']{.s-t1} 

[cognitive errors from Social-Proof Tendency. This]{.s-t1} 

[phenomenon was recently involved in a break]{.s-t1} 

[through by Judith Rich Harris who demonstrated that superrespect by young people for their peers,]{.s-t1} 

[rather than for parents or other adults, is ordained to some considerable extent by the genes of the young people. This makes it wise for parents to rely]{.s-t1} 

[more on manipulating the quality of the peers than]{.s-t1} 

[on exhortations to their own offspring. A person like Ms. Harris, who can provide an insight of this]{.s-t1} 

[quality and utility, backed by new reasons, has not]{.s-t1} 

[lived in vain.]{.s-t1} 

 

[And in the highest reaches of business, it is]{.s-t1} 

[not all uncommon to find leaders who display followership akin to that of teenagers . If one oil]{.s-t1} 

[company foolishly buys a mine, other oil companies often quickly join in buying mines. So also if the]{.s-t1} 

[purchased company makes fertilizer. Both of these]{.s-t1} 

[oil company buying fads actually bloomed, with bad]{.s-t1} 

[results.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Of course, it is difficult to identify and correctly]{.s-t1} 

[weigh all the possible ways to deploy the cash flow]{.s-t1} 

[of an oil company. So oil company executives, like]{.s-t1} 

[everyone else, have made many bad decisions that were quickly triggered by discomfort from doubt.]{.s-t1} 

[Going along with social proof provided by the]{.s-t1} 

[action of other oil companies ends this discomfort in]{.s-t1} 

[a natural way.]{.s-t1} 

 

[When will Social-Proof Tendency be most easily triggered. Here the answer is clear from many]{.s-t1} 

[experiments: Triggering most readily occurs in the]{.s-t1} 

[presence of puzzlement or stress, and particularly]{.s-t1} 

[when both exist.]{.s-t1} 

 

[Because stress intensifies Social-Proof]{.s-t1} 

[Tendency, disreputable sales organizations,]{.s-t1} 

[engaged, for instance, in such action as selling]{.s-t1} 

[swampland to schoolteachers, manipulate targets]{.s-t1} 

[into situations combining isolation and stress. The]{.s-t1} 

[isolation strengthens the social proof provided by]{.s-t1}
